### TAG-WM: Tamper-Aware Generative Image Watermarking via Diffusion Inversion Sensitivity
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.23484v1)
- **Authors**: Yuzhuo Chen, Zehua Ma, Han Fang, Weiming Zhang, Nenghai Yu
- **Abstract**: AI-generated content (AIGC) enables efficient visual creation but raises copyright and authenticity risks. As a common technique for integrity verification and source tracing, digital image watermarking is regarded as a potential solution to above issues. Among these, watermarking methods capable of preserving the generation quality are receiving increased attention. However, the proliferation and high performance of generative image editing applications have elevated the risks of malicious tampering, creating new demands. 1) The tamper robustness of current lossless visual quality watermarks remains constrained by the modification-sensitive diffusion inversion process, necessitating enhanced robustness. 2) The improved tampering quality and rapid iteration cycles render passive tampering detection methods inadequate, making proactive tampering localization capability a desired feature for watermarks. To address these requirements, this paper proposes a Tamper-Aware Generative image WaterMarking method named TAG-WM. The proposed method comprises four key modules: a dual-mark joint sampling (DMJS) algorithm for embedding copyright and localization watermarks into the latent space while preserving generative quality, the watermark latent reconstruction (WLR) utilizing reversed DMJS, a dense variation region detector (DVRD) leveraging diffusion inversion sensitivity to identify tampered areas via statistical deviation analysis, and the tamper-aware decoding (TAD) guided by localization results. The experimental results indicate that TAG-WM achieves SOTA tampering robustness and tampering localization capability with distortions while maintaining lossless generation quality and a considerable capacity of 256 bits.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "TAG-WM: Tamper-Aware Generative Image Watermarking via Diffusion Inversion Sensitivity" proposes a novel watermarking method for AI-generated content (AIGC) that not only embeds copyright information but also provides tamper localization capabilities. The method, called TAG-WM, leverages the sensitivity of DDIM inversion to pixel modifications. It comprises four key modules: a dual-mark joint sampling (DMJS) algorithm for embedding copyright and localization watermarks, watermark latent reconstruction (WLR), a dense variation region detector (DVRD) for identifying tampered areas, and tamper-aware decoding (TAD) guided by localization results.  Experimental results demonstrate state-of-the-art tampering robustness and tamper localization while maintaining lossless generation quality and a substantial capacity of 256 bits.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents several novel aspects.
    *   **Dual-Mark Joint Sampling (DMJS):** Embedding both copyright and localization watermarks simultaneously into the latent space of diffusion models, preserving generative quality. This is a practical approach, considering the growing need for both functionalities.
    *   **Dense Variation Region Detector (DVRD):** Using DDIM inversion sensitivity to identify tampered areas, leveraging the characteristics of AI image manipulation is a thoughtful approach, addressing the weaknesses of inversion-based methods against image manipulation distortions.
    *   **Tamper-Aware Decoding (TAD):** Using tamper localization to improve watermark decoding is an innovative way to improve robustness.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical problem in the AIGC era: copyright protection and authenticity verification. The increasing sophistication of AI-based image editing tools poses a significant challenge to existing watermarking techniques. TAG-WM tackles this by providing not just copyright protection but also the ability to pinpoint tampered areas. The method's performance, as demonstrated in the experiments, indicates a practical and effective solution. However, its reliance on DDIM inversion limits its generalizability to all diffusion model schedulers. Also, vulnerabilities to attacks that globally distort noise patterns are also worth attention.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addresses a relevant and timely problem:** The paper focuses on a pressing issue of copyright and authentication in AI-generated content.
    *   **Well-designed architecture:** The modular design of TAG-WM allows for a clear understanding of each component's contribution.
    *   **Strong experimental results:** The paper provides comprehensive experiments demonstrating the superiority of TAG-WM compared to existing methods.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on DDIM inversion:** The method's reliance on DDIM inversion limits its broader applicability to other diffusion model schedulers. More research should be done on generalizability to other types of diffusion processes.
    *   **Vulnerability to full-image adversarial attacks:** The vulnerability to adversarial attacks is a potential security concern that needs to be addressed.
    *   **Fixed seed for localization watermark**: Using a fixed seed may lead to vulnerabilities to malicious attacks, more advanced attacks may be conducted if the patterns are cracked.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of AIGC security. The ability to both protect copyright and detect tampering will be valuable for creators, platforms, and consumers. Future work should focus on extending the method's applicability to other generative models and addressing its vulnerabilities.

*   **Rigorous Rationale for Score:**

The paper is a solid contribution to the field. The proposed framework is well-motivated, technically sound, and experimentally validated. The DMJS, DVRD and TAD represent innovative approaches. While some limitations regarding the dependence on the DDIM inversion and image distribution shifts exist, the work significantly advances the state-of-the-art in generative image watermarking. It is practical, has the potential for real-world impact, and addresses an increasingly important issue in the AIGC landscape.

### LLM-enhanced Action-aware Multi-modal Prompt Tuning for Image-Text Matching
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.23502v1)
- **Authors**: Mengxiao Tian, Xinxiao Wu, Shuo Yang
- **Abstract**: Driven by large-scale contrastive vision-language pre-trained models such as CLIP, recent advancements in the image-text matching task have achieved remarkable success in representation learning. Due to image-level visual-language alignment, CLIP falls short in understanding fine-grained details such as object attributes and spatial relationships between objects. Recent efforts have attempted to compel CLIP to acquire structured visual representations by introducing prompt learning to achieve object-level alignment. While achieving promising results, they still lack the capability to perceive actions, which are crucial for describing the states or relationships between objects. Therefore, we propose to endow CLIP with fine-grained action-level understanding by introducing an LLM-enhanced action-aware multi-modal prompt-tuning method, incorporating the action-related external knowledge generated by large language models (LLMs). Specifically, we design an action triplet prompt and an action state prompt to exploit compositional semantic knowledge and state-related causal knowledge implicitly stored in LLMs. Subsequently, we propose an adaptive interaction module to aggregate attentive visual features conditioned on action-aware prompted knowledge for establishing discriminative and action-aware visual representations, which further improves the performance. Comprehensive experimental results on two benchmark datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of our method.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes an LLM-enhanced action-aware multi-modal prompt-tuning method to improve image-text matching, specifically addressing the limitations of CLIP in understanding fine-grained actions. The approach leverages external action knowledge from LLMs to create action triplet prompts (subject-action-object) and action state prompts, guiding the visual encoder to better capture action-related information. An adaptive interaction module is introduced to focus on salient action cues and reduce noise. The method is evaluated on COCO and Flickr30K datasets, demonstrating improved performance over existing CLIP-based models. A two-stage training strategy is used, first learning effective prompts, and then refining multi-modal interactions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the specific way the paper integrates LLMs into image-text matching for improved action understanding. While using LLMs for visual tasks and prompt tuning is not entirely new, the combination of action triplet and action state prompts derived from LLMs to guide visual representation learning for fine-grained action perception appears to be a novel contribution. The adaptive interaction module further refines the visual representation based on action-aware information.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a recognized limitation of CLIP: its weaker performance in tasks requiring fine-grained understanding of actions and relationships between objects. By enhancing CLIP with action knowledge derived from LLMs, the paper offers a promising approach to improve image-text matching performance, which is a fundamental task with broad applications. The experimental results demonstrate significant improvements, suggesting the method's potential to advance the state-of-the-art in this field.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear Problem Definition: The paper clearly identifies the limitations of CLIP in perceiving actions.
    *   Well-Defined Approach: The proposed method is well-structured, with clear explanations of the action triplet prompts, action state prompts, and adaptive interaction module.
    *   Comprehensive Experiments: The experimental evaluation is thorough, with comparisons to state-of-the-art methods and ablation studies to evaluate the effectiveness of each component.
    *   Significant Performance Improvements: The results show significant performance improvements over existing methods, demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed approach.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Dependence on LLM Quality: The performance of the method relies heavily on the quality of the action knowledge generated by the LLM. If the LLM generates inaccurate or irrelevant information, it could negatively impact the performance. While the paper explores different LLMs, the robustness of the approach to noise/errors in the LLM output is not thoroughly investigated.
    *   Computational Cost: The introduction of LLMs and the adaptive interaction module add computational overhead. The paper doesn't explicitly discuss the computational cost of the method, which may be a concern for resource-constrained applications.
    *   Qualitative Analysis Limitations:  The qualitative results highlighted a failure case where the LLM couldn't accurately capture a specific action state.  This indicates a vulnerability and suggests further investigation into LLM-derived prompt reliability is needed.

*   **Justification of Score:** The paper makes a novel and significant contribution to the field of image-text matching. It addresses a limitation of CLIP and proposes a well-designed and effective approach to enhance action understanding. The experimental results demonstrate significant improvements, suggesting the method's potential to advance the state-of-the-art. While the approach depends on the quality of the LLM and there isn't a large exploration of LLMs impact, the contribution is significant enough to give a score of 8.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Reinforcement Fine-Tuning Enables MLLMs Learning Novel Tasks Stably
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.23508v1)
- **Authors**: Zhihao Zhang, Qiaole Dong, Qi Zhang, Jun Zhao, Enyu Zhou, Zhiheng Xi, Senjie Jin, Xiaoran Fan, Yuhao Zhou, Yanwei Fu, Tao Ji, Tao Gui, Xuanjing Huang
- **Abstract**: Post-training algorithms such as Supervised Fine-Tuning (SFT) and Reinforcement Fine-Tuning (RFT) are widely used to adapt multimodal large language models to downstream tasks. While effective at task adaptation, their impact on prior knowledge remains unclear. In this paper, we introduce jigsaw puzzles as a novel task absent from existing pretraining corpora and systematically study the behavior of SFT and RFT on an open-source multimodal model, Qwen2.5-VL. Our experiments reveal a sharp trade-off: SFT enables rapid task acquisition but leads to catastrophic forgetting, whereas RFT learns more slowly on novel tasks but maintains prior knowledge. We analyze this phenomenon through the lens of learning dynamics, showing that RFT reinforces correct samples that are naturally aligned with the base model's probability landscape, mitigating interference with prior knowledge. Moreover, supervised training on correct RFT-simulated rollouts allows SFT to preserve knowledge while rapidly learning new tasks. These findings suggest that data distribution, rather than algorithmic differences, plays a central role in forgetting, and highlight RFT's potential for stable continual learning in multimodal large language models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the effects of Supervised Fine-Tuning (SFT) and Reinforcement Fine-Tuning (RFT) on the retention of prior knowledge in Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) when learning novel tasks. Using jigsaw puzzles as a novel task absent from pre-training corpora, the authors demonstrate that SFT, while enabling rapid task acquisition, leads to catastrophic forgetting of previous knowledge. In contrast, RFT learns more slowly but maintains prior knowledge. The paper attributes this difference to the data distribution: SFT datasets typically reside in low-probability regions of the base model's output space, while RFT leverages rollouts that are naturally aligned with the model's probability landscape.  The paper further shows that SFT trained on RFT-generated rollouts can achieve both rapid learning and knowledge preservation. A theoretical analysis based on learning dynamics is presented, linking the likelihood change of responses to gradients induced by training examples, providing a principled understanding of the interference and motivating future work on fine-tuning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates a well-known problem in a new context (MLLMs) and introduces a novel task (jigsaw puzzles) to systematically evaluate it. While catastrophic forgetting is a well-studied phenomenon, the paper's exploration in the context of MLLMs, and its connection to the data distribution used for fine-tuning, adds a new dimension. The observation that data construction is key, and the link to learning dynamics, also offers a valuable perspective.
*   **Significance:** The findings have important practical implications for the development and deployment of MLLMs. Understanding and mitigating catastrophic forgetting is crucial for enabling continual learning and adaptation to new tasks without sacrificing existing knowledge. The insight that RFT's avoidance of forgetting stems from leveraging data aligned with the base model's probability landscape provides a concrete avenue for improving fine-tuning strategies. Showing that SFT on RFT generated data avoids catastrophic forgetting is a very useful result.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Systematic Experimentation:** The paper presents a well-designed set of experiments that clearly demonstrate the different behaviors of SFT and RFT.
    *   **Theoretical Analysis:** The paper provides a solid theoretical grounding for the observed phenomena through a learning dynamics perspective.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the experimental setup, results, and analysis.
    *   **Practical Implications:** The paper offers actionable insights for improving fine-tuning strategies to mitigate forgetting.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Tasks:** While the jigsaw puzzle task is a good example, it might be argued that its specific characteristics influence the results. Expanding the study to a wider range of tasks and modalities would strengthen the generality of the findings.
    *   **Computational Cost of RFT:** The reliance on RFT for generating suitable training data, while effective, is computationally expensive. The paper would benefit from exploring more efficient methods for identifying or creating data distributions that avoid catastrophic forgetting.
    *   **Model Scope:** The experiments are confined to Qwen2.5-VL-3B. Applying this to larger models and different architectures would increase confidence in the paper's conclusions.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to the field of MLLMs by highlighting and analyzing the impact of post-training methods on knowledge retention. The novel task, systematic experiments, theoretical analysis, and actionable insights all contribute to the value of the work. The weaknesses related to the limited task scope and computational cost are important considerations. However, the findings offer a clear path towards more stable and effective fine-tuning strategies.

### ViewPoint: Panoramic Video Generation with Pretrained Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.23513v1)
- **Authors**: Zixun Fang, Kai Zhu, Zhiheng Liu, Yu Liu, Wei Zhai, Yang Cao, Zheng-Jun Zha
- **Abstract**: Panoramic video generation aims to synthesize 360-degree immersive videos, holding significant importance in the fields of VR, world models, and spatial intelligence. Existing works fail to synthesize high-quality panoramic videos due to the inherent modality gap between panoramic data and perspective data, which constitutes the majority of the training data for modern diffusion models. In this paper, we propose a novel framework utilizing pretrained perspective video models for generating panoramic videos. Specifically, we design a novel panorama representation named ViewPoint map, which possesses global spatial continuity and fine-grained visual details simultaneously. With our proposed Pano-Perspective attention mechanism, the model benefits from pretrained perspective priors and captures the panoramic spatial correlations of the ViewPoint map effectively. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method can synthesize highly dynamic and spatially consistent panoramic videos, achieving state-of-the-art performance and surpassing previous methods.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "ViewPoint: Panoramic Video Generation with Pretrained Diffusion Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel framework, "ViewPoint," for generating panoramic videos using pre-trained diffusion models. It addresses the challenge of generating high-quality panoramic videos due to the modality gap between panoramic and perspective data. The core contribution is a new panorama representation called the "ViewPoint map," designed to offer both global spatial continuity and fine-grained visual details. The framework incorporates a "Pano-Perspective attention mechanism" that leverages pre-trained perspective priors and captures panoramic spatial correlations. The authors demonstrate through experiments that their method can synthesize dynamic and spatially consistent panoramic videos, achieving state-of-the-art performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty lies primarily in the "ViewPoint map" representation and the Pano-Perspective attention mechanism. While the idea of using pre-trained diffusion models isn't entirely new, adapting them for panoramic video generation with a custom representation is a significant contribution. The ViewPoint map elegantly tackles the continuity vs. distortion trade-off of existing representations (ERP and Cubemaps). The Pano-Perspective attention further enhances the framework by addressing both global coherence and local fidelity, two areas that were not adequately addressed by prior approaches.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a relevant and growing area of research in immersive media, VR, and world models. The ability to generate high-quality, spatially consistent panoramic videos has significant implications for creating more realistic and engaging VR experiences, enabling more sophisticated world modeling, and advancing spatial intelligence. The paper's state-of-the-art results demonstrate the potential of the ViewPoint framework.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing panoramic video generation techniques and the challenges associated with the modality gap between perspective and panoramic data.
    *   **Technical Contribution:** The ViewPoint map is a well-designed and innovative approach for representing panoramic videos, balancing continuity and detail. The Pano-Perspective attention mechanism is also a valuable contribution, allowing the model to leverage pre-trained perspective priors while capturing spatial correlations.
    *   **Experimental Results:** The paper provides comprehensive experimental results, including qualitative comparisons, quantitative evaluations, and user studies, demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed method.
    *   **Clear Writing:** The paper is well-written and easy to understand, clearly explaining the technical details of the proposed framework.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependency on Pre-trained Models:** The framework relies heavily on the availability of pre-trained perspective video models. While this is a common approach, it limits the framework's applicability to domains where such models exist.
    *   **Computational Cost:** The paper mentions costly computational resources. More detailed discussion on model complexity (number of parameters, FLOPS) and training time would be beneficial. A comparison of the computational cost with other methods is necessary.
    *   **Limited Diversity:** While the method demonstrates impressive consistency and visual quality, there is not much discussion around the diversity of generated panoramic videos. The method seems to mainly focus on improving spatial coherence and leveraging input prompts or video, and it would benefit from showing how well the models handle the challenge of generating different scenes that are realistic at the same time.
    *   **No Error Analysis:** The paper doesn't offer an error analysis or highlight specific failure cases of the ViewPoint framework. Discussing the types of scenarios where the method struggles would further strengthen the paper.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the field of panoramic video generation by providing a new and effective approach for representing and generating panoramic videos. The ViewPoint map and the Pano-Perspective attention mechanism could be adopted by other researchers and practitioners in the field.

*   **Room for Improvement:** Future work could explore ways to reduce the computational cost of the framework, improve the diversity of generated videos, and address the dependency on pre-trained perspective models. Additionally, examining potential applications of the framework in VR and other immersive environments would be valuable.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

A score of 7 reflects the paper's solid contributions and potential impact, balanced against certain limitations.

*   It goes above the average primarily because of its elegant ViewPoint map and the Pano-Perspective attention, which represent genuine technical advancements that address real challenges in panoramic video generation. The improvement it shows in state-of-the-art performance over other approaches, and the good experimental results and writing style makes a strong impression.
*   It doesn't achieve a higher score because of its strong reliance on pre-trained models, which is a constraint, and lack of discussion regarding computational complexity and diversity. These limitations prevent it from being truly transformative or having wider adoption, and also highlights there is room for improvement. Furthermore, the absence of an error analysis and the absence of a strong demonstration on how the videos work on VR sets makes the paper solid but with limitations.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### WAVE: Warp-Based View Guidance for Consistent Novel View Synthesis Using a Single Image
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.23518v1)
- **Authors**: Jiwoo Park, Tae Eun Choi, Youngjun Jun, Seong Jae Hwang
- **Abstract**: Generating high-quality novel views of a scene from a single image requires maintaining structural coherence across different views, referred to as view consistency. While diffusion models have driven advancements in novel view synthesis, they still struggle to preserve spatial continuity across views. Diffusion models have been combined with 3D models to address the issue, but such approaches lack efficiency due to their complex multi-step pipelines. This paper proposes a novel view-consistent image generation method which utilizes diffusion models without additional modules. Our key idea is to enhance diffusion models with a training-free method that enables adaptive attention manipulation and noise reinitialization by leveraging view-guided warping to ensure view consistency. Through our comprehensive metric framework suitable for novel-view datasets, we show that our method improves view consistency across various diffusion models, demonstrating its broader applicability.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "WAVE: Warp-Based View Guidance for Consistent Novel View Synthesis Using a Single Image" introduces a training-free approach to improve view consistency in single-image novel view synthesis using diffusion models.  The key idea is to leverage 3D warping to guide the diffusion process without adding extra modules or requiring retraining. This is achieved through two main components: (1) warp-guided adaptive attention, which uses warped images to improve inter-view information flow, and (2) pose-aware noise initialization, which embeds pose information into the initial noise to reduce randomness. The method is evaluated using a newly proposed metric framework that combines video consistency metrics and camera pose accuracy. The results demonstrate improved view consistency and comparable image quality compared to existing diffusion-based and rendering-based methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its training-free approach to enhancing view consistency in diffusion models for novel view synthesis. While 3D warping and attention mechanisms have been used in other contexts, the specific combination of warp-guided adaptive attention and pose-aware noise initialization, tailored to address the inherent limitations of diffusion models without adding extra modules, is a novel contribution. The introduction of the novel view consistency metric combining video and camera pose accuracy is also a good idea.  However, the incremental nature of building upon existing diffusion architectures should be acknowledged.  The use of warping itself is not a novel technique in the field, but the way it's integrated within the diffusion process to guide attention *adaptively* is what lends the work its unique flavor.

* **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from its ability to improve view consistency without the computational overhead and complexity of multi-step pipelines that incorporate 3D models or large language models. It offers a practical approach to generate consistent novel views with relatively fast inference times. The proposed view consistency metric addresses a gap in the evaluation of novel view synthesis, providing a more comprehensive way to measure consistency beyond traditional reconstruction metrics. The demonstration of improved 3D rendering performance using the generated images further emphasizes the practical relevance of this work.  While the paper addresses a specific problem within novel view synthesis, the techniques presented could potentially be applied to other areas where consistent image generation is crucial, such as video generation or image editing.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Training-Free:** The fact that WAVE doesn't require additional training is a significant advantage, allowing it to be readily integrated with existing pre-trained diffusion models.
    * **Efficiency:**  The approach achieves competitive performance with significantly faster inference times compared to methods like VistaDream.
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The proposed metric framework addresses the limitations of existing evaluation methods for view consistency, providing a more reliable and comprehensive assessment.
    * **Clear Explanation:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the methodology, design choices, and experimental results.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Dependence on Warping Quality:** The method's performance is inherently limited by the quality of the 3D warping, and large viewpoint changes may lead to missing regions and reduced accuracy. The failure cases illustrate this limitation.
    * **Incremental Improvement:** While the approach is novel in its combination of techniques, it builds upon existing diffusion architectures and warping methods, which could be viewed as incremental.
    * **Limited Datasets:** The experimental evaluation is performed on a limited number of datasets, and further validation on more diverse and challenging scenes would strengthen the generalizability of the findings.
    * **Limited comparison:** A more comprehensive evaluation including more recent SoTA for novel view synthesis would further enhance the paper.

* **Potential Impact:** The paper offers a practical and efficient solution for improving view consistency in novel view synthesis, which could have a positive impact on various applications, including virtual reality, augmented reality, and 3D content creation. The proposed metric framework could also become a valuable tool for evaluating future research in this area. The training-free approach makes it easily adaptable and increases its likelihood of adoption. The simplicity of implementation could make it widely used as a plug-and-play solution.

**Score:** 7.5

**Justification:** The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of novel view synthesis by addressing a key challenge – view consistency – with a training-free and efficient approach. The combination of warp-guided adaptive attention and pose-aware noise initialization is novel and effective. The paper is well-written, the experiments are thorough, and the proposed metric framework is a valuable contribution. However, the dependence on warping quality, the incremental nature of the approach, the limited datasets and comparisons prevent a higher score. The relatively easy deployment and applicability of the method warrants a higher score in general, however the limitations and somewhat incremental novelty pushes the score slightly down.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### NEU-ESC: A Comprehensive Vietnamese dataset for Educational Sentiment analysis and topic Classification toward multitask learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.23524v1)
- **Authors**: Phan Quoc Hung Mai, Quang Hung Nguyen, Phuong Giang Duong, Hong Hanh Nguyen, Nguyen Tuan Long
- **Abstract**: In the field of education, understanding students' opinions through their comments is crucial, especially in the Vietnamese language, where resources remain limited. Existing educational datasets often lack domain relevance and student slang. To address these gaps, we introduce NEU-ESC, a new Vietnamese dataset for Educational Sentiment Classification and Topic Classification, curated from university forums, which offers more samples, richer class diversity, longer texts, and broader vocabulary. In addition, we explore multitask learning using encoder-only language models (BERT), in which we showed that it achieves performance up to 83.7% and 79.8% accuracy for sentiment and topic classification tasks. We also benchmark our dataset and model with other datasets and models, including Large Language Models, and discuss these benchmarks. The dataset is publicly available at: https://huggingface.co/datasets/hung20gg/NEU-ESC.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces NEU-ESC, a new Vietnamese dataset for educational sentiment and topic classification, designed to address limitations in existing resources like domain relevance and student slang.  The dataset, curated from university forums, features more samples, richer class diversity, longer texts, and a broader vocabulary. The authors explore multi-task learning using BERT-based models, demonstrating improved performance compared to single-task approaches and benchmarking against other datasets and LLMs.  The dataset is made publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Dataset Novelty:** The primary contribution is the creation and release of the NEU-ESC dataset.  The paper convincingly argues that existing Vietnamese educational datasets are limited in scope (formal, lacks slang), domain relevance (not focused on education), or scale.  NEU-ESC directly addresses these gaps, providing a valuable resource for the NLP community.
    *   **Multi-Task Learning:** The exploration of multi-task learning is a sound approach, given the inherent relationships between sentiment and topic. The paper shows that combining sentiment analysis and topic classification can enhance performance for both tasks.
    *   **Benchmarking:**  The paper thoroughly benchmarks performance against several baselines, including BERT variants and Large Language Models (LLMs). This provides a solid foundation for future research using the dataset.
    *   **Addressing Overfitting:** The inclusion of SMART loss regularization showcases an attempt to address a known issue in fine-tuning pre-trained language models.
    *   **Public Availability:** Making the dataset publicly available ensures reproducibility and facilitates further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Technological Novelty:** While the dataset is a significant contribution, the multi-task learning approach itself is not particularly novel. The use of BERT and the basic architecture choices are fairly standard.  The application of SMART loss, while beneficial, is an existing technique.
    *   **LLM Performance:** The relatively poor performance of LLMs in the described experiments raises some questions. This might indicate issues with prompt engineering or the specific LLMs used, rather than an inherent limitation of LLMs for these tasks. While the dataset may be well-suited to BERT, the LLM performance discrepancy needs further examination.
    *   **Dataset Imbalance:** The dataset is imbalanced. Although the paper acknowledges this, further investigation into techniques to mitigate the effect of data imbalance such as data augmentation or using weighted loss function during training could improve the performance.
    *   **Error Analysis:** Although the paper briefly touches on error analysis, a more detailed error analysis would provide valuable insights into the model's limitations and guide future improvements.
    *   **Practical Impact:** While understanding student sentiment is important, the paper could elaborate more on the potential practical applications of the developed system. How can the insights gained from this system be used to improve the educational experience?

*   **Significance:**
    *   The dataset fills a critical gap in Vietnamese NLP resources, providing a valuable tool for researchers and practitioners working on educational applications.
    *   The multi-task learning experiments provide useful baselines and demonstrate the potential benefits of this approach for sentiment and topic classification in the educational domain.
    *   The paper contributes to the growing body of research on using NLP to understand and improve the student experience.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper's primary strength is the creation and release of the NEU-ESC dataset, which has demonstrable value to the Vietnamese NLP community, along with benchmarking different BERT models and LLMs. However, the methodological approach (multi-task learning with BERT) lacks significant novelty, and the analysis of LLM performance could be more thorough. The practical applications also need to be further elaborated. Given these considerations, the paper represents a solid but not groundbreaking contribution. The dataset itself elevates the score, but the lack of significant technological innovation holds it back.

**Score: 6**

- **Score**: 6/10

### On Recipe Memorization and Creativity in Large Language Models: Is Your Model a Creative Cook, a Bad Cook, or Merely a Plagiator?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.23527v1)
- **Authors**: Jan Kvapil, Martin Fajcik
- **Abstract**: This work-in-progress investigates the memorization, creativity, and nonsense found in cooking recipes generated from Large Language Models (LLMs). Precisely, we aim (i) to analyze memorization, creativity, and non-sense in LLMs using a small, high-quality set of human judgments and (ii) to evaluate potential approaches to automate such a human annotation in order to scale our study to hundreds of recipes. To achieve (i), we conduct a detailed human annotation on 20 preselected recipes generated by LLM (Mixtral), extracting each recipe's ingredients and step-by-step actions to assess which elements are memorized--i.e., directly traceable to online sources possibly seen during training--and which arise from genuine creative synthesis or outright nonsense. We find that Mixtral consistently reuses ingredients that can be found in online documents, potentially seen during model training, suggesting strong reliance on memorized content. To achieve aim (ii) and scale our analysis beyond small sample sizes and single LLM validation, we design an ``LLM-as-judge'' pipeline that automates recipe generation, nonsense detection, parsing ingredients and recipe steps, and their annotation. For instance, comparing its output against human annotations, the best ingredient extractor and annotator is Llama 3.1+Gemma 2 9B, achieving up to 78% accuracy on ingredient matching. This automated framework enables large-scale quantification of memorization, creativity, and nonsense in generated recipes, providing rigorous evidence of the models' creative capacities.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates memorization, creativity, and nonsense in recipes generated by Large Language Models (LLMs). The authors aim to (i) analyze these aspects using human judgments on a small recipe set and (ii) automate the annotation process for larger-scale analysis.  They perform a detailed human annotation on 20 recipes generated by Mixtral, identifying memorized elements (traceable to online sources), creative synthesis, and nonsense.  They find that Mixtral heavily relies on ingredients found in online documents, indicating memorization. To scale the analysis, the authors develop an "LLM-as-judge" pipeline to automate recipe generation, nonsense detection, parsing, and annotation. Preliminary results with Llama 3.1+Gemma 2 9B show promising ingredient matching accuracy compared to human annotators.  The work-in-progress outlines an annotation approach to quantify memorized and creative elements in recipes that both humans and LLMs can follow.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel combination of approaches.  While recipe generation and LLM evaluation are not entirely new, the focus on distinguishing memorization from creative synthesis in the specific context of cooking recipes, coupled with the development of an automated annotation pipeline, provides a fresh perspective. The "LLM-as-judge" system, although preliminary, offers a potentially scalable method for analyzing model creativity. The specific annotation schema with detailed categories like "Found (Not Exact Wording)", and "Tool Implied" adds a layer of sophistication. The legal considerations also indicate attention to the broader implications.
*   **Significance:** Understanding the creative capabilities and limitations of LLMs is crucial. Identifying memorization is important to prevent plagiarism.  The ability to automatically assess recipe quality is potentially useful for applications that leverage LLMs for cooking assistance or recipe recommendations. The focus on scaling the analysis is valuable, as it addresses a significant challenge in LLM evaluation.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The human annotation provides a solid ground truth for evaluating the automated system.
    *   The detailed annotation schema is well-defined and captures nuances in memorization and creativity.
    *   The "LLM-as-judge" pipeline is a practical approach to scaling the analysis.
    *   The paper addresses an interesting and relevant problem with potentially broad implications.
    *   The inclusion of a discussion of the legal ramifications of model output adds an important dimension often overlooked.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The analysis is currently limited to a small set of recipes and a single LLM (Mixtral) for the initial assessment.
    *   The "LLM-as-judge" pipeline is still in its early stages. The low task accuracy observed with some models might limit the reliability of the automated annotation.
    *   The definition of "creativity" as simply "not found in available documents" is somewhat simplistic.
    *   The exhaustivity analysis only hints at the need for more examination. It provides no conclusive evidence of whether or not these models use creative synthesis.
    *   The pipeline is not yet fully automatized. The "LLM-as-judge" methodology is not yet fully realized as the automated pipeline is still dependent on human evaluation.
    *   The pipeline only provides an indication that the model may be plagiarizing. It does not prove plagiarism beyond a reasonable doubt.

*   **Justification of Score:** The paper presents a well-defined research question and a novel methodology for analyzing the creative capacities of LLMs in the specific domain of recipe generation. While the research is in progress and has some limitations, the preliminary results are promising and the "LLM-as-judge" pipeline offers a potentially significant contribution to scalable LLM evaluation. The analysis of the legal framework provides interesting implications. The current stage of development and the inherent limitations temper the potential influence of the work.
Score: 6
'Score: 6' The study offers a compelling approach to a relevant problem. While the work is a work-in-progress and is somewhat limited in scope, it provides the basis for a more complete understanding of the topic as the authors work on automation and testing.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Comparative Analysis of the Code Generated by Popular Large Language Models (LLMs) for MISRA C++ Compliance
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.23535v1)
- **Authors**: Malik Muhammad Umer
- **Abstract**: Safety-critical systems are engineered systems whose failure or malfunction could result in catastrophic consequences. The software development for safety-critical systems necessitates rigorous engineering practices and adherence to certification standards like DO-178C for avionics. DO-178C is a guidance document which requires compliance to well-defined software coding standards like MISRA C++ to enforce coding guidelines that prevent the use of ambiguous, unsafe, or undefined constructs. Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated significant capabilities in automatic code generation across a wide range of programming languages, including C++. Despite their impressive performance, code generated by LLMs in safety-critical domains must be carefully analyzed for conformance to MISRA C++ coding standards. In this paper, I have conducted a comparative analysis of the C++ code generated by popular LLMs including: OpenAI ChatGPT, Google Gemini, DeepSeek, Meta AI, and Microsoft Copilot for compliance with MISRA C++.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the ability of Large Language Models (LLMs) to generate C++ code that adheres to the MISRA C++:2008 standard, a set of coding guidelines crucial for safety-critical systems. The author analyzes the output of five popular LLMs (ChatGPT, DeepSeek, Gemini, Meta AI, and Copilot) when prompted to create checksum algorithms compliant with MISRA C++. Using the static analysis tool PC-lint Plus, the paper identifies and compares the number and types of MISRA violations in the generated code. The results indicate that none of the LLMs produce fully compliant code, highlighting limitations in their ability to enforce strict coding standards required for safety-critical applications. The paper also explores the ability of these LLMs to identify and correct MISRA violations when provided with specific prompts. The findings suggest that while LLMs can be useful for initial code generation, they require careful review and rigorous static analysis to ensure compliance with safety-critical coding standards.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Relevant Problem:** The paper addresses a highly relevant and practical problem: the reliability of LLM-generated code in safety-critical domains. As LLMs are increasingly used in software development, ensuring their compliance with strict coding standards like MISRA C++ is essential.
    *   **Clear Methodology:** The methodology is well-defined and clearly explained. The paper describes the LLMs used, the prompt given, the static analysis tool employed, and the process for analyzing and comparing the results.
    *   **Comparative Analysis:** The comparative analysis of different LLMs is valuable. It provides insights into the relative strengths and weaknesses of each model in generating MISRA-compliant code.
    *   **Violation Correction Investigation:** The additional experiment of querying for fixing the errors is a valuable investigation and shows how capable the LLMs are for fixing given error in the code.
    *   **Practical Implications:** The paper's findings have practical implications for developers working on safety-critical systems. It emphasizes the need for caution when using LLMs and highlights the importance of thorough static analysis and manual review.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Code Examples:** While checksum algorithms are commonly used, the paper focuses on a relatively narrow range of code examples. Expanding the analysis to more complex and diverse safety-critical code would strengthen the findings.
    *   **Reliance on a Single Static Analysis Tool:** Using only PC-lint Plus as the static analysis tool could introduce bias. Comparing the results with other SATs would increase the robustness of the analysis. It is not guaranteed that PC-lint Plus accurately finds all of the violations that exist in the generated code.
    *   **Black-Box Approach to LLMs:** The paper treats LLMs as black boxes, without delving into the internal mechanisms that contribute to their compliance or non-compliance. This limits the ability to provide deeper insights into the underlying reasons for the observed results.
    *   **Subjectivity of "Correction Prompt" Success:** The method of correction of MISRA C++ violations relies on subjective analysis. More objective or quantitative success metrics may be appropriate to test for success.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The paper has a good degree of novelty since it tests the current general purpose LLMs for compliance with highly specific coding standards such as MISRA C++.
    *   The experiment to fix errors using a prompted instruction has been implemented successfully, and provides insight to the software engineers on how to use LLMs to generate error-free codes.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by:

    *   Raising awareness about the limitations of LLMs in generating safety-critical code.
    *   Motivating further research into techniques for improving the compliance of LLM-generated code with coding standards.
    *   Informing the development of tools and methods for integrating LLMs into safety-critical software development workflows.
    *   Helping software engineers to be cautious while implementing LLMs in safety-critical development scenarios.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper makes a solid contribution by rigorously evaluating and comparing the ability of existing LLMs to produce MISRA C++:2008 compliant code. The paper has a highly relevant and interesting investigation that will provide the software development community more in-depth knowledge of using LLMs to generate error-free code, especially when adhering to coding standards such as MISRA C++. I'm assigning a **Score: 7** because it addresses an important practical problem with a clear methodology, but the scope is somewhat limited, and the black-box approach to LLMs prevents deeper insights. Future work can build on this foundation by expanding the scope of code examples, using multiple static analysis tools, and exploring the internal mechanisms of LLMs.

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 7/10

### Uncertainty-aware Diffusion and Reinforcement Learning for Joint Plane Localization and Anomaly Diagnosis in 3D Ultrasound
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.23538v1)
- **Authors**: Yuhao Huang, Yueyue Xu, Haoran Dou, Jiaxiao Deng, Xin Yang, Hongyu Zheng, Dong Ni
- **Abstract**: Congenital uterine anomalies (CUAs) can lead to infertility, miscarriage, preterm birth, and an increased risk of pregnancy complications. Compared to traditional 2D ultrasound (US), 3D US can reconstruct the coronal plane, providing a clear visualization of the uterine morphology for assessing CUAs accurately. In this paper, we propose an intelligent system for simultaneous automated plane localization and CUA diagnosis. Our highlights are: 1) we develop a denoising diffusion model with local (plane) and global (volume/text) guidance, using an adaptive weighting strategy to optimize attention allocation to different conditions; 2) we introduce a reinforcement learning-based framework with unsupervised rewards to extract the key slice summary from redundant sequences, fully integrating information across multiple planes to reduce learning difficulty; 3) we provide text-driven uncertainty modeling for coarse prediction, and leverage it to adjust the classification probability for overall performance improvement. Extensive experiments on a large 3D uterine US dataset show the efficacy of our method, in terms of plane localization and CUA diagnosis. Code is available at https://github.com/yuhoo0302/CUA-US.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel framework for automated plane localization and Congenital Uterine Anomaly (CUA) diagnosis in 3D ultrasound images. The framework combines a denoising diffusion model with reinforcement learning (RL) to achieve simultaneous plane localization and anomaly detection. Key aspects of the approach include: (1) an adaptive denoising diffusion model utilizing both local (plane) and global (volume/text) guidance with adaptive weighting; (2) an RL-based framework with unsupervised rewards to extract key slice summaries; and (3) text-driven uncertainty modeling to refine classification probabilities. The method is evaluated on a large 3D uterine US dataset, demonstrating improved performance compared to existing approaches for both plane localization and CUA diagnosis.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Novel Combination:** The paper's key strength lies in its integration of a denoising diffusion model and reinforcement learning for the task of joint plane localization and CUA diagnosis. This combined approach seems to be a novel contribution.
    *   **Adaptive Denoising:** The adaptive weighting strategy for local and global guidance in the diffusion model is a valuable addition, allowing the model to focus on relevant information at different stages of the denoising process.
    *   **RL for Key Slice Selection:** The RL-based framework with unsupervised rewards offers an efficient way to extract key slices from redundant image sequences, reducing the computational burden and potentially improving the model's focus on informative regions.
    *   **Uncertainty Modeling:** The use of text-driven uncertainty modeling to refine classification probabilities is a good way to improve the robustness and reliability of the diagnosis, especially when dealing with ambiguous cases.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The method is evaluated on a reasonably large dataset and compared against several competitive baselines. The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of each component.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental improvements:** While the paper highlights novel combinations of techniques, the individual components (diffusion models, RL) are well-established. The level of innovation might be questioned if the improvements over existing methods are not substantial.
    *   **Complexity:** Combining two complex models (diffusion and RL) can make the system difficult to train and fine-tune. The paper could benefit from a more detailed discussion of the training process and hyperparameter selection.
    *   **Lack of novelty in RL rewards:** The reinforcement learning reward is a linear combination of common metrics like the classification loss and the average similarity between slices. It is not clear how well these rewards capture the characteristics of 'key slices'. The reward function is relatively straightforward, lacking a more sophisticated understanding of the medical task.
    *   **Ambiguity in uncertainty quantification:** The paper suggests that using the correct text condition will lead to less uncertainty. This seems overly simplified, especially considering the inherent ambiguity in medical imaging and diagnosis. It is not always clear how the text prompts relate to actual uncertainty in the image.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper addresses an important clinical problem (CUA diagnosis) and provides a potentially useful tool for assisting clinicians.
    *   The joint approach to plane localization and diagnosis has the potential to streamline the diagnostic workflow.
    *   The paper could inspire further research on the integration of diffusion models and RL for medical image analysis tasks.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The paper's influence will depend on the replicability of the results and the generalizability of the method to other medical imaging modalities and tasks.
    *   The code availability on GitHub is a positive factor that could facilitate adoption and further development by other researchers.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel combination of techniques, integrating diffusion models and RL for joint plane localization and CUA diagnosis in 3D US images. It incorporates clever features like adaptive denoising, RL for key slice selection, and uncertainty-aware adjustment. The method shows improvement over existing approaches. However, the lack of significant novelty in the individual components (the reward function in RL, the simplified text-based uncertainty quantification) and the incremental nature of the results hold it back from receiving a higher score.

### Pyramidal Patchification Flow for Visual Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.23543v1)
- **Authors**: Hui Li, Baoyou Chen, Liwei Zhang, Jiaye Li, Jingdong Wang, Siyu Zhu
- **Abstract**: Diffusion transformers (DiTs) adopt Patchify, mapping patch representations to token representations through linear projections, to adjust the number of tokens input to DiT blocks and thus the computation cost. Instead of a single patch size for all the timesteps, we introduce a Pyramidal Patchification Flow (PPFlow) approach: Large patch sizes are used for high noise timesteps and small patch sizes for low noise timesteps; Linear projections are learned for each patch size; and Unpatchify is accordingly modified. Unlike Pyramidal Flow, our approach operates over full latent representations other than pyramid representations, and adopts the normal denoising process without requiring the renoising trick. We demonstrate the effectiveness of our approach through two training manners. Training from scratch achieves a $1.6\times$ ($2.0\times$) inference speed over SiT-B/2 for 2-level (3-level) pyramid patchification with slightly lower training FLOPs and similar image generation performance. Training from pretrained normal DiTs achieves even better performance with small training time. The code and checkpoint are at https://github.com/fudan-generative-vision/PPFlow.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Pyramidal Patchification Flow for Visual Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Pyramidal Patchification Flow (PPFlow), a technique to accelerate diffusion transformers (DiTs) for visual generation.  PPFlow modifies the patchification process in DiTs by using larger patch sizes for high-noise timesteps and smaller patch sizes for low-noise timesteps. Linear projections are learned for each patch size. This allows for a reduction in the number of tokens processed during the denoising process, thus speeding up inference. The method operates on full latent representations, avoiding the pyramid representations used in prior work (Pyramidal Flow) and the associated renoising tricks.  The paper presents results from training from scratch and fine-tuning pre-trained DiTs, demonstrating improved inference speed with comparable or even better FID scores.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using variable patch sizes based on noise level is relatively straightforward, but the key lies in its effective implementation and comparison with existing techniques.  The authors explicitly distinguish their approach from Pyramidal Flow, highlighting that PPFlow operates on the *full* latent representation rather than pyramid representations. This simplifies the denoising process and avoids renoising tricks needed in Pyramidal Flow. However, adapting the original Patchify process to have a variable-sized representation is a marginal contribution in novelty.

*   **Significance:** Improving the inference speed of diffusion models is significant because their computational cost is a limiting factor in their wider adoption.  PPFlow achieves a notable speedup (1.6x-2.0x) with minimal impact on image quality, as measured by FID. The fact that the method can be applied both when training from scratch and when fine-tuning existing models increases its practical value. Additionally, using pre-trained DiTs further reduces training time. However, the actual performance numbers, while an improvement in the metrics reported, are incremental.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear presentation:** The paper is well-written and the approach is clearly explained, along with a comprehensive comparison to existing methods.
    *   **Experimental validation:** The authors provide thorough experimental results, comparing PPFlow to baseline DiTs and Pyramidal Flow under various training scenarios.
    *   **Practical benefit:** The technique demonstrably improves inference speed without significantly sacrificing image quality, which is a valuable contribution to the field.
    *   **Code release:** The authors have made the code and checkpoints available, which is crucial for reproducibility and wider adoption.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental improvement:** While the results are positive, the performance gains are relatively modest, especially with state-of-the-art DiTs. The core idea is not dramatically different from prior works that aim to reduce computational complexity by decreasing tokens in the process.
    *   **Limited Scope:** The paper focuses on class-conditional image generation. Applying it to text-to-image tasks might require different design choices.

*   **Potential Influence:** PPFlow's ease of implementation and its ability to be applied to existing DiT architectures could lead to its adoption in various visual generation applications. The simple yet effective nature of the technique makes it an appealing candidate for further research and optimization.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a reasonably novel and practical approach to accelerating diffusion transformers, achieving a meaningful speedup without sacrificing image quality. While the incremental contribution is modest, the clarity, thorough evaluation, and code release all strengthen its potential impact.
However, the core idea (using varying patch sizes) is not entirely new and builds upon previous work, and the performance improvements, although present, are also incremental. A careful analysis of computational impact compared to model size is also missing.

### CooT: Learning to Coordinate In-Context with Coordination Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.23549v1)
- **Authors**: Huai-Chih Wang, Hsiang-Chun Chuang, Hsi-Chun Cheng, Dai-Jie Wu, Shao-Hua Sun
- **Abstract**: Effective coordination among artificial agents in dynamic and uncertain environments remains a significant challenge in multi-agent systems. Existing approaches, such as self-play and population-based methods, either generalize poorly to unseen partners or require extensive training. To overcome these limitations, we propose Coordination Transformers (CooT), a novel in-context coordination framework that uses recent interaction histories to adapt to unseen partners rapidly. Unlike previous approaches that primarily aim to increase the diversity of training partners, CooT explicitly focuses on adapting to new partner behaviors by predicting actions aligned with observed partner interactions. Trained on interaction trajectories collected from diverse pairs of agents with complementary behaviors, CooT quickly learns effective coordination strategies without explicit supervision or fine-tuning. Evaluations on the Overcooked benchmark demonstrate that CooT significantly outperforms baseline methods in coordination tasks involving previously unseen partners. Human evaluations further confirm CooT as the most effective collaborative partner, while extensive ablations highlight its robustness, flexibility, and sensitivity to context in multi-agent scenarios.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "CooT: Learning to Coordinate In-Context with Coordination Transformers":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Coordination Transformers (CooT), a novel framework for enabling artificial agents to adaptively coordinate with unseen partners in multi-agent environments. CooT leverages in-context learning by conditioning on recent interaction histories to predict best-response actions, thereby eliminating the need for explicit task generalization or reliance on diverse training populations, unlike self-play or population-based methods.  The framework is trained on trajectories between behavior-preferring agents with distinct underlying preferences, maximizing the ability of CooT to adapt effectively without explicit supervision or fine-tuning.  The authors evaluate CooT on the Overcooked benchmark, demonstrating its superiority over baseline methods like HSP, MEP, and BC in both agent-agent and human-agent coordination scenarios. Extensive ablation studies highlight CooT's robustness, flexibility, and sensitivity to temporal context.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its application of in-context learning specifically to the problem of partner generalization in multi-agent coordination. While in-context learning has been explored in other RL contexts, its adaptation to handle the nuances of inferring and adapting to diverse partner behaviors (rather than just different task objectives) within a shared environment is a clear contribution. The method does not merely apply an existing ICL technique, but tailors it to tackle specific multi-agent coordination challenges, notably, zero-shot coordination.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant challenge in multi-agent systems: how to achieve effective coordination with previously unseen partners.  Traditional methods often require extensive training or are prone to conventions that fail to generalize.  CooT offers a potentially more efficient and adaptable solution by directly learning context-driven strategies. The empirical results on Overcooked are compelling and demonstrate clear improvements over strong baselines. The human evaluation adds further weight to the method's practical relevance. The paper is particularly relevant as AI systems are increasingly deployed in collaborative settings with humans and other AI agents, where adaptability is key.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges of partner generalization in multi-agent coordination.
    *   **Technically Sound:** The proposed CooT architecture appears well-designed, leveraging transformers for in-context learning in a sensible way.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The experiments on Overcooked are extensive, covering a variety of layouts and baselines, which enhance credibility. The inclusion of human evaluations is a significant strength.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies are insightful, providing a better understanding of CooT's key components and their contributions.  The investigation into trajectory augmentation techniques is particularly valuable.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Environment Complexity:** While Overcooked is a widely used benchmark, it remains a relatively structured and simplified environment compared to more open-ended real-world scenarios.  The generalization capabilities of CooT in more complex and dynamic environments remain to be seen.
    *   **Passive Partner Assumption:** CooT primarily focuses on adapting to the behavior of partners without explicitly modeling their adaptation in return.  Future work should examine scenarios where both agents are actively adapting.
    *   **Lack of theoretical analysis:** Although they experiment on a complex multi agent system, the paper lacks theoretical analysis to justify the selection of the design choices.
*   **Potential Impact:** The paper's findings could have a significant impact on the development of more adaptable and collaborative AI systems. CooT's approach may inspire new methods for partner generalization in other multi-agent settings, including robotics, human-AI collaboration, and automated negotiation. The in-context learning paradigm, tailored for coordination, opens doors for few-shot adaptation in settings where extensive training is impractical.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and well-evaluated approach to partner generalization in multi-agent systems. The results are convincing, and the human evaluation adds to the practical significance. While the limitations related to environment complexity and the passive partner assumption are important, the paper makes a strong contribution to the field. However, there are some limitations in the environments, as the world dynamics and action space of the agents is limited by design.

### A unified framework on the universal approximation of transformer-type architectures
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.23551v1)
- **Authors**: Jingpu Cheng, Qianxiao Li, Ting Lin, Zuowei Shen
- **Abstract**: We investigate the universal approximation property (UAP) of transformer-type architectures, providing a unified theoretical framework that extends prior results on residual networks to models incorporating attention mechanisms. Our work identifies token distinguishability as a fundamental requirement for UAP and introduces a general sufficient condition that applies to a broad class of architectures. Leveraging an analyticity assumption on the attention layer, we can significantly simplify the verification of this condition, providing a non-constructive approach in establishing UAP for such architectures. We demonstrate the applicability of our framework by proving UAP for transformers with various attention mechanisms, including kernel-based and sparse attention mechanisms. The corollaries of our results either generalize prior works or establish UAP for architectures not previously covered. Furthermore, our framework offers a principled foundation for designing novel transformer architectures with inherent UAP guarantees, including those with specific functional symmetries. We propose examples to illustrate these insights.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a unified theoretical framework for analyzing the universal approximation property (UAP) of transformer-type architectures. It identifies token distinguishability as a crucial requirement for UAP and introduces a general sufficient condition that applies to a wide range of architectures, including those with attention mechanisms like kernel-based and sparse attention. The authors leverage an analyticity assumption on the attention layer to simplify the verification of the sufficient condition. They demonstrate the applicability of their framework by proving UAP for various transformer variants, generalizing prior work and establishing UAP for previously uncovered architectures. The paper also offers a principled basis for designing new transformer architectures with UAP guarantees, including those preserving functional symmetries.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**  The paper's primary contribution lies in providing a *unified* framework for UAP analysis in transformers.  Previous works often relied on architecture-specific constructions, making it difficult to generalize results.  The "token distinguishability" condition, while intuitively reasonable, provides a formal criterion that is applicable across different attention mechanisms. Simplifying the verification process using analyticity assumptions is also a significant improvement over brute-force construction approaches. The framework allows for the principled design of architectures to maintain particular properties like permutation equivariance, which is novel in its own right. While the individual techniques used (e.g., leveraging analyticity, relying on universal approximation results for feedforward networks) might not be entirely new, their combination within this specific context and the resulting unification represent a tangible advance.

**Significance:** The significance of this work resides in its ability to provide a more general and easily verifiable condition for UAP. This has both theoretical and practical implications:

*   **Theoretical:** It deepens our understanding of the expressive power of transformers by abstracting away from specific architectural details and identifying fundamental requirements. The work moves beyond just demonstrating existence of UAP to providing a framework by which one can understand _why_ a given architecture will (or will not) possess the property.

*   **Practical:** The framework simplifies the design process for new transformer architectures.  Instead of having to explicitly construct a UAP-achieving network, designers can focus on ensuring token distinguishability, leading to more flexible and potentially more efficient architectures. The ability to design for specific symmetry requirements is also highly valuable in applications where such symmetries are important (e.g., crystal structure prediction). However, the assumption of analyticity in some attention mechanisms could limit the applicability of the proposed framework in cases where this assumption is not valid.

**Strengths:**

*   **Generality:** The framework is applicable to a wide range of transformer architectures, including those with different attention mechanisms.
*   **Verifiability:** The analyticity assumption simplifies the verification of the UAP condition.
*   **Principled Design:** The framework offers guidance for designing new transformer architectures with UAP guarantees.
*   **Extends Previous Work:** The framework generalizes prior results and establishes UAP for previously uncovered architectures.
*   **Addresses symmetry:** Considers more general symmetry groups than previous research.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Analyticity Assumption:** The analyticity assumption might not hold for all attention mechanisms, limiting the framework's applicability in certain cases.
*   **Normalization layers:** The analysis doesn't account for layer normalization, which is frequently found in these networks.
*   **Token Distinguishability Condition**  While simplified by the analyticity argument, the token distinguishability condition itself can be difficult to directly verify in some cases, despite being less construction-based.
*   **Practical impact** The practical benefits of the theoretical framework for designing architectures are difficult to know without further experimental validations.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence the design and analysis of future transformer architectures.  It provides a solid theoretical foundation for understanding and guaranteeing expressive power, which could lead to the development of more efficient and robust models. It may also lead to the development of better tools and techniques for verifying UAP in transformers. The design guidance for equivariant transformers can have an impact on areas such as materials science, where symmetry plays a critical role.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:** The paper represents a significant advance in the theoretical understanding of transformers. The unified framework and simplified verification process are valuable contributions to the field, offering a step change in moving towards an understanding of why transformers work. Although limitations remain, such as the analyticity assumption and the handling of normalization layers, the work's generality and potential impact justify a score of 7. While not revolutionary, it is a strong and impactful contribution. Further work validating the design guidelines and the framework's applicability to architectures beyond those considered would strengthen the overall impact.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### MMReason: An Open-Ended Multi-Modal Multi-Step Reasoning Benchmark for MLLMs Toward AGI
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.23563v1)
- **Authors**: Huanjin Yao, Jiaxing Huang, Yawen Qiu, Michael K. Chen, Wenzheng Liu, Wei Zhang, Wenjie Zeng, Xikun Zhang, Jingyi Zhang, Yuxin Song, Wenhao Wu, Dacheng Tao
- **Abstract**: Reasoning plays a crucial role in advancing Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) toward Artificial General Intelligence. However, existing MLLM benchmarks often fall short in precisely and comprehensively evaluating long-chain reasoning abilities from three key aspects: (1) lack of difficulty and diversity, (2) susceptibility to guessability and memorization, (3) inadequate assessment of intermediate reasoning steps. To fill this gap, we introduce MMReason, a new benchmark designed to precisely and comprehensively evaluate MLLM long-chain reasoning capability with diverse, open-ended, challenging questions. First, we curate challenging questions requiring multi-step reasoning from various fields (i.e., 6 disciplines) and multiple difficulty levels (i.e., from pre-university to university, and from foundational to competition tiers). Second, these questions are reformulated into an open-ended format and filtered using a multi-model voting technique to eliminate shortcut cases related to guessing and memorization, ensuring robust reasoning evaluations. Third, we annotate the questions with detailed step-by-step solutions, and design a reference-based ternary scoring mechanism to reliably assess intermediate reasoning steps. With MMReason, we benchmark popular leading MLLMs and provide an in-depth analysis of their reasoning capabilities. We hope MMReason will serve as a valuable resource for advancing MLLM reasoning research. Code will be available at https://github.com/HJYao00/MMReason.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MMReason, a new multi-modal, multi-step reasoning benchmark designed to evaluate the long-chain reasoning capabilities of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). The benchmark addresses limitations in existing benchmarks by: (1) curating more challenging and diverse questions spanning multiple disciplines and difficulty levels, (2) mitigating guessability and memorization issues through data filtering strategies (reformulating multiple-choice questions to open-ended ones and using a multi-model voting technique to filter out memorized or visually irrelevant questions), and (3) providing a reference-based ternary scoring mechanism to evaluate intermediate reasoning steps. The paper benchmarks several popular MLLMs on MMReason and presents an analysis of their reasoning capabilities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its holistic approach to addressing the shortcomings of existing MLLM benchmarks. While individual aspects like creating challenging datasets or evaluating intermediate steps have been explored previously, MMReason combines these into a single, comprehensive benchmark. The multi-model voting approach for mitigating memorization is a practical and effective contribution. Converting multiple-choice questions to open-ended is an established technique, but the paper executes it well within the context of a broader evaluation strategy. The detailed annotation of intermediate steps and the reference-based ternary scoring mechanism enhance the robustness and reliability of the evaluation process. The collection of data from diverse areas also contributes to the overall novelty of the paper.

**Significance:**  The paper addresses a critical need in the field of MLLM research: the need for more rigorous and comprehensive benchmarks.  As MLLMs become increasingly sophisticated, existing benchmarks are proving inadequate at accurately measuring their reasoning abilities.
